<page-num>160</page-num>
<header>Fifth Part.</header>

and these are not all one and the same. The reward <note>original: "lon"</note> lies in the work, the dignity in the virtues, and the crown in love <note>original: "miñe," referring to mystical, divine love</note>. But the reward is rich according to the variety of good works; the dignity is expanded according to the measure of the virtues; the crown shines in the heights according to the diligence of the burning in love. Brother Albert told me then that a brother was to die after six years. That did not come true. In the seventh year, I asked our Lord how that could be. Then our Lord spoke: "He saw the decree <note>or "the natural order/disposition"</note> and not my election. I elect my special friends into long, undeserved humiliation, and I preserve them in holy longing to live longer."

Whenever a person looks into their own heart in the light of love—that is, in the truth—they find nothing except that they should rightly be more despised than anyone else. Therein the longing grows with immeasurable hunger and then brings the person out of themselves into God's will so far that God deigns to preserve the person and then gives them entirely new gifts, if they will keep and protect them with good diligence.

### XXIX.
*According to God’s drawing <note>the "tug" or "pull" of divine grace</note>, a person would be like an angel if they followed it. And concerning the malice of the devil.*

Whoever held themselves rightly according to the drawing that comes from God and according to the light that they recognize, they would exist <margin>Manuscript: "keñe" <note>likely a scribal variant for "kenne" or "kame"</note></margin> in such great joy and in such holy knowledge as no heart could bear. Then they would be like an angel, at all times lovingly united with God in all things. Then they would become the devil’s hell and God’s kingdom of heaven. But whenever the good person lets go of that drawing, God sends the devil to them, that he may tempt them with the things that are most difficult, in order that He may awaken them again. But our dear Lord withdraws the power from the devil and protects the person so that he cannot make them fall. Rather, the devil truly fancies that permission has been given to him to make the person fall according to his will; therefore he is so diligent day and night.

Oh, woe is me, poor soul! This has happened to me very often. God has shown me such an honorable thing and promised to fulfill it,

because of my unworthiness, I did not dare to trust it, and therefore, alas, I did not give Him thanks for it. Then the devil came and wanted to inflict pain upon me. Then I said, "What do you want? Surely you see well that God is here with me. How do you dare to torment me in His presence?" Then the devil said: "I want now, as I have always wanted, to set my throne beside His; indeed, I would drive Him from the throne of your soul if I could and seat myself therein, and I would wish that the kingdom of heaven, paradise, purgatory, and the earth were all one single hell within the eternal hell." Then I said: "Would you not rather that all these things were a kingdom of heaven, so that you too might return to grace?" Then he said: "No, that I can never do." Then I said: "Oh woe, how are you so truly wretched that you are not ashamed before God!" Then he said: "Whoever has any good in him is not entirely evil, and whoever sins loses his shame; for if he were ashamed, he would not commit the sin. I am as bold as a fly and always keep attacking. I spare no one; only he who defends himself with virtues remains unburdened, and he who stands firm in God honorably overcomes all his heartache."

Love, your departing and your coming,
Both are equally welcome
To the well-ordered soul.
Love, you have subjected everything
That God has begun with us in heartfelt love.
Love, your most noble purity,

Before God in the chaste soul,
Creates hot desire of love
In the virginal breast
Toward Jesus, her beloved.
Those who love greatly and are maidens,

Love, your holy mercy
Causes the devils much sorrow.
Love, your most sweet peace
Brings gentle spirits and pure customs.

## 31. Concerning the ten powers of love and how no creature can fully comprehend the soul's longing for God.

O Love, how broad your light becomes within the soul, and how fiery is your radiance, and how incomprehensible is your wonder, and how manifold is your wisdom, and how swift is your gift, and how powerful is your bond, and how perfect is your being, and how gentle is your flow, and how great is your value, and how faithful is your labor, and how holy is your discernment! When you traverse the soul with all these things, and she then lifts herself up and begins to fly with dove’s wings <note>A common medieval metaphor for the soul's ascent, referencing Psalm 55:6</note>—that is, with all the virtues—and then begins to long with the eagle’s greed <note>original: "aren girheit"; here meaning a holy, intense hunger or soaring desire</note>, she follows the heat up to heaven, for everything else seems cold and unsavory to her—

Yes Mary, rejoice, Mother of God! How would it be for you if you began to make known to your Son the love that a united soul, without falsehood, has for the eternal Godhead in this life, and the touch with which He caresses her? Lady, you might grow weary and your Son would have to become faint, for the fiery power of divine love <note>original: "gôtlichen mîne"; Minne is the Middle High German term for sacred, courtly love</note> goes beyond all human strength.

## XXXII. Concerning the noble end of Sister Mechthild.

Now I must write these words under constraint,
Which I would gladly have kept silent,
For I fear very much
The secret impulse of empty honor.
But I fear much more,
Should God judge me rightly,
That I have kept too much of God's wonders hidden.

Lamentation, fear, and constant heartache have I carried secretly since childhood concerning a good end <note>meaning a "good death" in a state of grace</note>. Now, in my final days, God has shown me this: that from the kingdom of heaven came four hosts in procession; these were virgins and angels. The virgins signified the virtues with which a person has served God. The angels signified a pure life, in which the person has followed God.

Our Lord and His honorable Mother
Followed the wondrous procession,
Until the first ones stood
Before the person's mouth.
How peaceful the way was all around,
How much brighter than the sun
From the radiance of the saints,
Who came there from God's bliss.

Then the soul spoke: "Lord, this way pleases me immensely beyond all my worthiness, but I fear greatly how I shall come out of my body." Then our Lord spoke: "When that is to happen, I will draw in my breath, so that you follow me

as a jet stone <note>original: "agestein"; likely referring to black amber or jet, used here to describe a dark, solid appearance</note>. On both sides of the procession was a host of devils; there were so many of them that it was impossible for me to see past them, yet I feared none of them. They struck at one another with great fury and clawed at each other like madmen. The soul rejoiced in this even more than in seeing our Lord before her. Then, out of great wonder, she asked our Lord how this could be. Our Lord spoke: "This joy comes from the certain security that all these devils may never hinder you from me."

## XXXIII. *How small sins hinder perfection and how the devil approaches the soul through them.*

That which hinders spiritual people most of all from true perfection is that they pay so little heed to small sins. I tell you truly: whenever I neglect myself with a laugh that harms no one, or with a bitterness in my heart that I show to no one, or with a small impatience regarding my own pain, my soul becomes so dark, and my senses so dull, and my heart so cold, that I must weep miserably and lament piteously, and ask in friendship and desire with power and humbly confess all my faults; only then can grace happen for me, poor as I am, so that I may creep back like a beaten dog into the kitchen.

Furthermore, whenever I have a flaw in me that is unacknowledged and unchanged, a hellish stain immediately appears on my soul. Then there can be no remedy. The devil, who tends the purgatory <note>original: "vegefûres"; the cleansing fire where sins are burned away</note> where the sin ought to burn, immediately wants to look upon his own likeness. Then I begin to shudder when I am alone, even though my soul was made free from all shuddering when I received the gift called "acknowledged love" <note>original: "bekantû miñe"; a technical term for a conscious, reciprocal love between the soul and God</note>. Then I immediately fall upon the earth and say: <margin>Have mercy on me, O God <note>Latin: *miserere mei deus*</note> or Our Father <note>Latin: *pater noster*</note>.</margin> Then I immediately come back into my sweet paradise, from which the stain had shown me out.

<summary>The author describes a vision of defeated devils and warns that even minor spiritual negligences, like a careless laugh or hidden bitterness, can darken the soul and invite the devil's influence.</summary>

# Part Five

### XXXIV.
Concerning five kinds of new saints, sent because of wicked people, and how God intends to wash Christendom in His own blood hereafter. <note>original: "Von fünfleie núwe heligen..." This refers to the contemporary 13th-century saints who were seen as divine interventions in a failing church.</note>

I marvel greatly at the nobility that lies within holiness and at the weakness that lies within humanity. Our Lord instructed me regarding how Saint Elizabeth <note>St. Elizabeth of Hungary (1207–1231), a contemporary of the author known for her extreme charity.</note> became holy so quickly and lay for so short a time beneath the earth, and He spoke thus: "It is the right of messengers that they be swift. Elizabeth is and was a messenger whom I sent to the wretched ladies who sat in their castles, so saturated with unchastity, so covered over with pride, and so constantly surrounded by vanity, that by rights they should have gone into the abyss. Many a woman has followed her example, to the extent that they were willing and able."

"I sent Saint Dominic <note>Founder of the Dominican Order (Order of Preachers).</note> as a messenger to the unbelieving and as a teacher to the ignorant and as a comfort to the sorrowful. I also sent Francis <note>St. Francis of Assisi, founder of the Franciscan Order.</note> as a messenger to the greedy clergy <term>pfaffen: a term for priests, often used pejoratively in this period to denote worldly clergy</term> and to the proud laity. Furthermore, Saint Peter <note>St. Peter of Verona (Peter Martyr), a Dominican inquisitor assassinated in 1252.</note>, the new martyr, is my messenger of blood, in which false Christendom is now so miserably entangled. They all say they are pure, yet before my eyes they are unchaste. They say they are faithful, yet before my eyes they are false. They say they love me, yet they love their own flesh much more. Whoever wishes to remain with me, let them—like Saint Peter—renounce the earthly body. Hidden guilt creates, in the end, manifest distress."

I, a poor human, was so bold in my prayer that I acted audaciously; I took all of corrupted Christendom into the arms of my soul, and there I labored with lamentation. Then our Lord spoke:

# Chapter 34.

When poor Christendom came thus before our Lord, she was like a young maiden. Then I looked upon her, and I saw also that she looked upon our Lord. At this, I was greatly ashamed. Then our Lord spoke: "Now behold, is it fitting for me to love this maiden in my eternal bridal bed without end, and to take her to me with my imperial arms, and to gaze upon her with my divine eyes, seeing that she is <term>blear-eyed</term><note>original: "surôgge"; referring to dimmed or diseased vision, implying a lack of spiritual clarity</note> in her understanding, and she is also lame in her hands, for she hardly performs any good works? She is also limping in the feet of her desires, for she thinks of me but seldom and sluggishly. She is also filthy of skin, for she is unclean and unchaste." Then the poor spirit spoke: "What counsel shall then be given to her?" Then our Lord spoke: "I will wash her in her own blood, and all the blessed who are truly innocent, I will protect them and take them secretly to myself in a holy death."

Furthermore, our Lord spoke: "Sister Jutta of Sangerhausen <note>A 13th-century mystic and patron saint of Prussia who lived as a recluse and cared for the poor</note> I have sent as a messenger to the heathens with her holy prayer and her good example." Our Lord also said this: "I send this book now as a messenger to all spiritual people, both the evil and the good. For when the pillars fall, the work cannot stand. I tell you truly," spoke our Lord, "in this book stands written my heart’s blood, which I will pour out a second time in the final days." Our Lord spoke to me of three kinds of blood in this way: The first blood—that of Abel and the children <insert>murdered by Herod</insert>, John the Baptist, and all those who shed their holy innocent blood before the martyrdom of our Lord—that was Christ’s blood, for they suffered blessed death for the sake of His love. The second blood was the blood of the Heavenly Father, which Christ poured out from His innocent heart. The third blood, which shall be shed for the Christian faith before the Last Day, is the blood of the Holy Spirit; for without the devotion of the Holy Spirit, no good deed was ever accomplished. The blood of the martyrs for Christ gives fellowship and boldness; the Father’s blood in Christ gives...

### XXXV.
How Sister Mechthild thanks and praises God and prays for three kinds of people and for herself.

->And flow toward her, Lord, with everything<-
->That You possess wonderfully within Yourself.<-

O Father of all goodness, I, a poor sinner, thank You for all Your faithfulness with my tormented body and with my wretched soul and my sinful heart and with my troubled senses and with my despised existence in this world. Lord Father, that is mine and nothing else; and [I thank You] with Your dear Son Jesus Christ, and with the fellowship of all creatures. All of them were once without evil, and they shall return again in the most praiseworthy state that they wish to and can be.

O sweet Father, with all these things I praise You today for all Your faithful protection which You ever bestowed <note>original: "geletest" (laid/placed)</note> upon my poor body and upon my wretched soul. With these things, Great God, I thank You, Lord, for all Your merciful gifts which You, Lord, ever deigned to give me in body and soul. With this community <note>original: "meine" or "gemeine"</note> of all creatures, I desire today from my heart Your praise in all things, for all things, which You, Lord Father, have poured out unstained from Your sweet heart. But with all these things, Beloved above all beloveds, I pray to You, Lord, for Your own honor, for the true transformation and for the complete conversion of the poor sinners who today lie in the...

...mortal sins. I ask You further, my true Love, for the holy growth of all virtues and for Christian perseverance for all the blessed who live here without mortal sins.

I also ask You, most Beloved, for all tormented souls who have passed into purgatory <note>original: "vegefůr"</note> because of our sins, whom we should have protected through our good example. I ask You, Lord, for holy healing and for true protection, and for the fulfillment of Your Holy Spirit for all those by name who help me, Lord, in my misery <note>original: "ellende," which implies both wretchedness and the state of being an exile from heaven</note> to bear my poverty in body and soul for the sake of Your love. I ask You, rich God, through Your poor son Jesus, that You would turn the pain of my spiritual poverty and the gall of my bitterness into honey upon the palate of my soul. I ask You, living God, for the eternal nobility of our Christian faith, that You, Lord, may then protect us from all false witnesses with Your divine wisdom. And steady our spirit, Lord, to rest in Your holy Trinity.

I ask You, sweet Lord, for all those who persecute Christians <term>cristanpinger: literally "Christian-tormentors," those who cause suffering to the faithful</term>, that they may yet come to acknowledge You and love You in a holy way. I ask You, almighty God, for true governance with discernment <note>original: "stůrunge mit vnderscheide"</note> for the false people in power, and for merciful sparing <margin>The manuscript uses the spelling: schŏnunge.</margin> of the innocent among the common people. I ask You, eternal Comfort, that You would come today to comfort all those troubled souls who must depart from their bodies today in anguish, so that You, merciful God, would be their savior and judge them into eternal life. I ask You, Lord, for pure cleansing and spiritual perseverance, and the praiseworthy preservation of divine truth in all things, for all those by name who bear a spiritual appearance and spiritual authority <note>original: "geistlichen schin und geistlichen gewalt"</note> solely for the sake of Your love. I ask You, gracious God, for true gratitude at all times for all Your gifts, to help those who bear heavy burdens for the sake of Your love.

I ask You, holy God, for a merciful look upon my useless life and for a constant union, Lord, of Yourself within my soul, and for the faithful food-for-the-journey <term>wegespise: Viaticum, the final reception of the Eucharist before death</term> of Your holy body, that it may be my final food at my end.

## This is the sixth part of this book.

---

### *I. How a prior or prioress or other prelates should conduct themselves toward their subjects. The first chapter.*

Great fear lies in the possession of power. When people say: "You are now our prelate," or "our prior," or "our prioress," God knows, dear person, you have been put to the highest test. Therefore, you should with great humility perform your prostration <note>original: "venie." A ritual of bowing or lying prostrate on the ground, common in monastic life to show submission to God's will.</note> and go at once to your prayers and let God comfort you. Then you should transform your heart in holy divine love, so that you love each brother or sister who has been entrusted to you especially in all their needs. 

You should be with your subjects and brothers in a way that is lovingly joyful or kindly serious, and you should be merciful toward all their labor. With sweet words you should command them to go out to preach boldly and to hear confessions piously; for God has sent them into this world so that they should be redeemers <term>lỏsere: literally "looseners" or "liberators," referring to the priest's power to "loose" a person from their sins</term> and helpers of poor sinners <note>original: "sůndern"</note> in the same way that Christ was the redeemer of the whole world and came down from the high palace of the holy Trinity into this swampy world.

Thus you should speak to every brother with the bottomless humility of your pure heart:

> Oh, dear person, I who am unworthy of all good,
> I am your servant with all service
> As far as I am able, and not your master.

<summary>Mechthild begins the sixth book by offering spiritual counsel to those in positions of religious authority, emphasizing that leadership should be characterized by humility, service, and a maternal/paternal love for one's subordinates.</summary>
<keywords>leadership, humility, prior, prioress, redemption, service, monastic life, Trinity</keywords>

--- Page 204 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for religious superiors (priors, prioresses, or prelates) begun on the previous page. Mechthild provides a specific script for how a leader should address a brother being sent out on a mission, emphasizing that the leader is a servant rather than a master.</meta>

# Sixth Part.

Alas, I have authority over you,
And with heartfelt divine love I send you out.
I have great pity for your labor,
Yet I maintain this distinction.
I rejoice in the highest dignity
Which the Heavenly Father has prepared for you.
Now I send you in that same name
In which Jesus went from His Father,
When He sought the one lost sheep
For so long that He died of love <note>original: "miñe"</note>.
True divine love must guide you
On holy ways and in useful labors.
I wish to send with you the longing of my soul and my heart’s prayer,
And the tears of my sinful eyes,
So that God, holy and full of love,
May send you back to me in love. Amen.

Thus you should comfort all your brothers <note>The manuscript uses the word *trůsten*, meaning to comfort or console.</note> as they go out;
You should also gladden them when they return.
You should go before them into the guesthouse <term>gasthus: a place for receiving visitors and travelers</term>
And arrange through God’s generosity
For the disciples of God every necessary comfort
That you are ever able to provide.
Oh, dear person, you yourself should wash their feet;
You still remain the master or mistress
And yet you are humbly their servant.
You should not stay long with the guests;
You should tend to the order of the community <term>Conventes: the organized body of monks or nuns</term>;
The guests should not stay awake late;
That is a holy matter.
Every day you should go into the infirmary <term>siechhus: literally "sick-house," the monastery's medical wing</term>
And anoint them with comforting words of God
And refresh them generously with earthly things,
For God is rich above all costs.
You should always keep things clean around the sick
And you should laugh with them sweetly in God.
You should personally carry away their private waste <note>original: "heimlichea notdurft." This refers to the humble task of cleaning bedpans or chamber pots, emphasizing that no task is too lowly for a spiritual leader.</note>
And faithfully and lovingly ask them
What their secret sicknesses might be,
And then truly stand by them;
Then God’s sweetness will flow into you.
You should also go into the kitchen and see to it
That the necessities for the brothers of the community are of good quality,
So that your stinginess and the cook's laziness <note>original: "kochestragheit"</note>
Do not steal from our Lord.

# Chapter 1.

> The sweet song in the choir—
> For a starving priest <note>original: "pfaffe"</note> never sings beautifully;
> Furthermore, a hungry man cannot study deeply,
> Thus, because of the worst [neglect], God must often lose the best.

In the chapter meeting <term>capittele: the formal daily gathering of the monastic community for business and confession</term>, you should be just with a sweet spirit and judge therein according to the fault with equality. You should take great care that you do not follow your own power against the will of the brothers or the will of the community <term>convent: the organized body of monks or nuns</term>, for from that comes great discord. You should always bless yourself against thoughts of pride, which unfortunately fall into the heart with a good appearance and say: "Indeed, you are after all above them all as prior or prioress; you might well do whatever seems good to you." No, dear person, with that you break the holy peace of God <term>gotzvriden: literally 'God's peace', the harmonious state of a religious community</term>. You should speak with a humble demeanor and with loving joy this: "Dear brother (or sister), how does this please you?" and then arrange your actions according to their best will.

Whenever your brothers or your sisters of your community offer you honor, you should fear within yourself with sharp guardianship of your heart and should feel shame outwardly with modest behavior. You should receive all complaints mercifully and give all counsel faithfully. If your brothers wish to build high <note>This likely refers to extravagant or tall stone buildings, which many reform-minded mystics saw as a distraction from spiritual life.</note>, you should turn them from it in a holy way and speak thus: "O, very dear brothers, we wish to build a delightful palace for the Holy Trinity in our souls with the timber of Holy Scripture and with the stones of noble virtues." The first stone of this honorable palace—where the eternal God shall forever woo His love-delightful bride <note>In Mechthild's mysticism, the "bride" is the human soul.</note> according to His powerful pleasure and her fragrant longing—is bottomless humility. This stone is so well-sealed with the sweet satisfaction of earthly transience that greedy pride and cutting vainglory can never exert their power so much that we build anything like earthly lords or ladies; rather, we want to build like heavenly princes on earth. Thus we shall sit on the Last Judgment day beside the poor Jesus, like the lord apostles. Dear brothers, we want to build our heavenly dwelling with divine joy, and we want to build our earthly shelter <note>original: "schůlunge," suggesting a place of learning or temporary lodging</note> with care and moderation, for we have no certain span of time to live even until tomorrow.

# Part Six.

You should have an eagle’s eye to notice and watch over your subordinates in God, lovingly and not with malice. If you find someone who is secretly tempted <note>original: "bekoret," meaning tested or tempted by spiritual trials</note>, oh, stand by them with all love; for God cannot allow it—He must be close to you if you are close to them.

To the blessed brothers who have a specific office <term>ambaht: a formal duty or administrative role within the monastery</term>, I wish to tell this true word which I saw in the Holy Trinity while I was in my wretched prayer <note>original: "ellendigen gebete." In Middle High German, "ellendig" carries the sense of being in "exile" or "abroad," reflecting the soul’s feeling of being a stranger on earth while longing for its true home in God</note>. Whenever a person prays in the Christian faith with a heart so humble that he cannot bear to find any creaturely distraction within himself, and with a soul so "exiled" that all things must withdraw from his prayer except God alone, then he is a **divine god** with the heavenly Father. Yet the person then considers best how truly lowly he is in himself; he also fears himself so much in that sweet embrace <term>vmbehalsunge: the mystical embrace between the soul and God</term> that nothing is known to him except the honor of God. But when the person works with right usefulness, out of true necessity, with that same love with which he prayed, then he is a **human god** with Christ. However, everything that one fusses over <note>original: "clůtteret," likely referring to trivial busywork or unnecessary construction</note> and labors at without usefulness and without necessity is all dead before God. Only when a person—for the love of God and not for earthly reward—teaches the ignorant, converts the sinner, comforts the troubled, and brings the despairing back to God, then he is a **spiritual god** with the Holy Spirit.

Oh, the most blessed person who does all things that are praiseworthy to God and possible for humans to do—if <insert>he</insert> does them in equal love to the praise of God with a steady intention of his whole heart, then he is a complete person with the Holy Trinity. But the dust of sin, which falls upon us as easily as [it falls] without our will, is turned to nothing by Love <term>miñe: the high, courtly, and divine love</term> as quickly as our soul’s eye turns to touch the Godhead with that wretched, sighing, sweet longing which no creature can resist. When the soul begins to rise, the dust of sin falls away from her; then she becomes one god with God, so that what He wills, she wills, and they cannot be united in any other way than with a whole union.

Oh human, you should always give our dear Lord God one free hour during the day or the night, in which you may pray lovingly without hindrance, for the heavenly gifts with which God...

<summary>Mechthild describes how a soul becomes "one god with God" by mirroring the Trinity through prayer, practical service, and spiritual guidance. She emphasizes that while sin is like unavoidable dust, it is instantly dissolved by the soul's longing for divine union.</summary>
<keywords>Holy Trinity, deification, mystical union, prayer, spiritual leadership, Mechthild of Magdeburg, Minne, active vs. contemplative life</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Mechthild’s spiritual instructions, transitioning from the external duties of monastery offices to the intimate, internal preparation required for mystical union with God. She moves from the "human god" active in service to the "divine god" found in the "bride-bed" of prayer.</meta>

### Chapter I.

...is accustomed to greet and teach His beloved chosen ones; it is by nature so noble and so delicate and flows so sweetly when the eternal God wishes to enter the secret bride-bed <term>notlich brutbette: literally a 'necessary' or 'urgent' bed, referring to the private place of spiritual union between the soul and God</term> of the soul longing for love. For He is so deeply wounded by her love <term>miñe: the Middle High German term for mystical, divine love modeled on courtly romance</term> that He has renounced all things for more than thirty years <note>original: "drissig jâr." This likely refers to the years of Christ's earthly life and passion, spent in pursuit of humanity's soul</note> which were fitting for Him, so that He might kiss her thoroughly and embrace her with His bare arms. If you would reflect on this, how could you be so boorish! You ought to give Him one hour a day in return for those thirty years.

Whenever I, the poorest of all people, go to my prayer, I adorn myself according to my unworthiness <term>vnedelkeit: literally "un-nobility," the base or lowly state of the human soul</term>; I clothe myself with the mire that I myself am. Then I put on my shoes *(shoe myself)* with the noble time that I have lost all my days, and I gird myself with the pain that I have deserved. After that, I wrap around me a cloak of the wickedness with which I am filled. Then I set upon my head a crown of the secret shame <term>schemede: a profound sense of spiritual embarrassment or modesty</term> which I have committed against God. Following this, I take in my hand a mirror of true self-knowledge <term>bekantnisse: the honest recognition of one's own sins and nature</term>, and I look into it to see who I myself am; and there, alas, I see nothing other than "Woe is me!"

These garments are much dearer to me to wear than having all earthly goods according to my heart's desire; and yet they are also so painful to me in wretched impatience that I would rather be clothed with hell and surrounded by all the devils without my guilt. Alas, how often the robbers of inconstancy <term>vnstetekeit: spiritual instability or fickleness</term> come and take these garments from us when we ¹) please ourselves and declare ourselves innocent of our guilt; then we are robbed of empty honors and struck down by pride <term>homůt: arrogance or haughtiness</term>, and we are more naked than the naked. Oh, how greatly we must then be ashamed before God and before His friends and before all creatures! If we wish to overcome all our shame with great honor, we must clothe ourselves with ourselves in this way. Thus adorned, I seek Jesus my sweet Lord, and I find Him by no means as quickly [any other way]; even though these garments are heavy and cumbersome. One should step forward quite bravely with powerful longing,

---

¹) Manuscript: us. <note>The translator notes a correction from "vn" to "vns" (us/ourselves).</note>

# Sixth Part

...and with due shame and with flowing love and with humble fear, the filth of sins vanishes before the divine eyes of our Lord. Then He begins to shine lovingly upon the soul, and she begins to flow with heartfelt love. There the soul loses all her guilt and all her misery, and He begins to teach her all His will. Then she begins to taste His sweetness and He begins to greet her with His divinity, so that the power of the Holy Trinity <term>drivaltekeit: the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit</term> completely permeates her soul and her body. There she receives true wisdom <term>wisheit</term>, and He begins to cherish her so tenderly that she becomes weak <note>original: "krank." In this context, it refers to a spiritual fainting or ecstasy where the soul's natural strength fails in the presence of God</note>. 

Then she begins to suckle <note>original: "sugende." A common medieval metaphor where the soul draws spiritual nourishment directly from God, much like an infant</note> until He becomes love-sick <term>miñesiech: a state of being "sick with love," a recurring theme in bridal mysticism</term>. Then He begins to temper the measure <note>original: "die masse ze temperende." God regulates the intensity of the divine encounter so the human vessel is not destroyed</note>, for He knows her capacity better than she knows it herself. Then she begins to desire to maintain great faithfulness toward Him, and He begins to give her full knowledge <term>bekantnisse: deep spiritual recognition or discernment</term>. Then she begins to joyfully taste this even in her flesh through His love, and He begins to confirm all His gifts within her soul with holy will. If she then guards herself against the ignoble love of her flesh and against the greedy sweetness of all earthly things, she may love <term>miñen: to love with divine, mystical "Minne"</term> perfectly and God may gain much praise through all things.

Now, dear person, there are still two things that you should guard yourself against with holy diligence, for they never brought forth holy fruit. The first is when a man or woman desires to perform many good works and maintain good customs in devotion specifically so that they might be chosen as a prelate <term>prelaten: a high-ranking church official or the superior of a monastery</term>. My soul is hostile toward such an intention. When such people finally seize power, their vices become so manifold that no one finds comfort in the person they once chose with such great longing. Then such a person is cast out from honor, and their false virtues are turned into a scandal.

The second thing is if a person is laudably chosen for office without any fault of their own, but then they transform so much that they never desire to leave that position of authority <note>original: "korunge." Literally "the choosing" or election to office</note>. This is a sign of many vices. Even if a person is praiseworthy in their office, they should nonetheless always remain in fear and humble themselves. A true woman and a good man who would like to speak with me after my death, but cannot, should read this little book <term>bůchelin: Mechthild’s humble term for her own work</term>.

<summary>Mechthild describes the intimate physical and spiritual sensations of the soul's union with God and warns against the spiritual pride of seeking or clinging to religious offices. She concludes by offering her book as a means of "conversation" with her after her death.</summary>
<keywords>mystical union, divine sweetness, love-sickness, moderation, spiritual ambition, prelates, humility, Mechthild's book</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the general description of the soul’s mystical union with God to a specific, practical "rule of life" dictated by God for a particular individual—a canon. The previous page's discussion of "tempering the measure" of divine love flows here into the specific discipline required to sustain such a spiritual state in the world.</meta>

Chapter 2

### 11. Concerning the rule for a canon, how he should conduct himself. It has come from God.

We should greet people in the Holy Spirit with His divine assistance <term>volleiste: help, support, or spiritual abundance</term> and we should be grateful for their merciful gifts. But we should thank the Heavenly Father even more, in the fellowship of all creatures, for His holy gift which He pours from His Holy Trinity <term>drivaltekeit</term> into the hearts of sinners from day to day and without ceasing. — That the eagle flies so high, he does not need to thank the owls <note>This is a characteristic Mechthild metaphor, likely defending her high mystical insights against critics or "low-flying" intellectuals.</note>.

I prayed for a certain lord at his request <note>original: "gerunge." This suggests a specific nobleman or high-ranking cleric asked Mechthild to intercede for him.</note>. This is the holy answer from God, and He speaks thus to me:
> His desire is sinking toward a humble life, and My gift which I give him is great, and his will is holy; yet he should remain exactly where he is.

God, the High Pope of the Kingdom of Heaven, has sent him this rule and speaks thus:
> He should pray always, as if unceasingly, according to the priestly order <term>pfaeflicher ordenunge: the prescribed liturgical hours and duties of a priest or canon</term>. In addition, I will give him My divine sweetness, which he shall enjoy in the solitude <term>einôte</term> of his heart. Whenever he is tempted, he should call upon Me powerfully, and I will help him quickly <term>snelleklichen</term>. He should pay his debts completely and keep his personal expenses small. He should keep no one in his service for the sake of status or for hire, but rather he should keep only honest messengers for his genuine needs. He should not burden himself with his kinsmen; however, if one of them wished to follow his way of life, he should help them. He should wear clothes as light as those he wears now; but against his skin he should clothe himself with a rough garment <note>original: "hertem gewande." A reference to a hair shirt or coarse cloth used for penance.</note> to counter the many sweetnesses he has received in his flesh. He should also sleep on straw between two woolen cloths, and he should have two pillows under his head, and during the day he should spread a beautiful quilt over his bed, and his bed should stand in the same place where it stood before, in the open. A mat should lie before his bed, along with a bed-step. Thus, with a humble heart, he should provide a good example against a wicked life. He should also have two rods <note>original: "besmen." Literally "brooms," but here referring to bundles of twigs used for self-discipline or flagellation.</note> by his bed, with which he may discipline himself when he wakes.

<meta>H. Mechthild.</meta>
<sig>12</sig>

<summary>Mechthild records a specific "rule of life" received from God for a canon, balancing internal mystical "sweetness" with external acts of penance, humility, and frugality.</summary>
<keywords>Canon, Holy Trinity, Rule of Life, Penance, Solitude, Humility, Discipline, Prayer</keywords>

# Sixth Part

Every day, at one time, this shall be his prayer during his long prostration <term>venie: a ritual of kneeling or lying prostrate in prayer</term> as follows:

> Lord, eternal Father, God of the kingdom of heaven, I, an unworthy human, thank you, Lord, that you have inclined your grace toward me. Now I pray to you, very dear Father, by all your wonders, that your sweet dew from heaven <note>original: "himel val," literally 'heaven-fall,' referring to divine grace descending like dew</note>, which pours down without ceasing from the bottomless, living wellspring of your entire Holy Trinity <term>drivaltekeit</term>, may cleanse my soul without ceasing from all stains: *through our Lord.* <note>original Latin: "per dom nostrum," an abbreviation for the standard liturgical conclusion "per Dominum nostrum Jesum Christum."</note>

After this I asked: Lord, how should he conduct himself without sin in earthly honor? Then our Lord spoke: he should conduct himself with constant fear, just like a mouse sitting in a trap <term>vallen</term> and waiting for its death. The lowest part of the trap is this earthly honor; the uppermost part is my almighty power. The explanation <term>glosa</term> our Lord speaks: Whoever desires that I taste right to him <note>Meaning the person seeks to truly experience God's sweetness</note>, he should at all times and in all things shudder before the spark of his flesh, where the heart plays with secret lust. Therefore, when he eats, he should be moderate and kind; when he sleeps, he should be disciplined and alone with me. When he is with the world, he should be like a mouse in his heart <note>This implies a state of watchful, trembling humility even in public</note>. When he confesses, he should be truthful and obedient and accomplish all things with the advice of his confessor.

### III. God gives lordship. How the goats become lambs. <margin>concerning the aforementioned canon of Magdeburg</margin>

That this lord himself has been chosen as lord dean <term>techan: the head of a cathedral chapter</term> is God's will, for He himself has spoken thus: "Therefore I have moved him from one stall <note>A "stall" refers to the assigned seat for a canon in the cathedral choir, indicating his promotion from a regular canon to the dean</note> to another, so that he shall be a nourishment for the goats." Explanation <term>glosa</term>: That God calls the cathedral canons <term>tůmeherren</term> "goats" <term>bôke</term> is because their flesh stinks of unchastity in the Eternal Truth, before His Holy Trinity. The goat's hide is noble; so it is with their lordship and their prebend <term>pfrůnde: the land or income attached to a church office</term>. But when this hide is stripped away at death, it has lost all its nobility.

And our Lord God was asked how these goats <term>bôke</term> might become lambs <term>lamber</term>. Then our Lord spoke thus: "If they wish..."

<summary>Mechthild records a specific daily prayer and behavioral rule for a cathedral dean, using the metaphor of a mouse in a trap to describe the spiritual danger of earthly honors. She further critiques the corruption of cathedral canons, calling them "goats" due to their impurity, while noting their high social status is as temporary as a hide.</summary>

Cap. III—IV. 179

...if they eat the fodder that Lord Dietrich <note>Mechthild frequently uses the name "Dietrich" (Theodoric) as a symbolic name for Christ, representing Him as a noble protector and provider.</note> lays in the manger for them, which is holy penance and faithful counsel in confession, then they shall become a kind of lamb called rams—lambs with horns. Those horns are spiritual power <term>spiritual power: ecclesiastical authority or the "potestas" of a priest</term>, which they should use holily for the praise of God. One should be strong and trust God completely, for He says: I myself will help pay the lord's debt with good fortune.

### IV. *Concerning discernment and fear, which protect the senses from earthly things.*

Alas, poor me! I complain to God of the kingdom of heaven that I am now worse than I was thirty years ago; for the creatures <note>original: "creaturen," referring to all created, worldly things or physical comforts</note> that helped me bear my misery <term>misery: the state of "ellende," or spiritual exile while living in the world</term> then did not need to be so noble, if the poor body was to recover. Therefore, I must without ceasing set two guardians between my soul and all earthly things, so that they do not taste any more to my flesh than my poor necessity requires. They also guard my senses, so that these earthly things do not lead me astray into a greed for having much or using it for a long time. 

The first guardian is discernment <term>discernment: the medieval virtue of "bescheidenheit" or *discretio*, meaning the ability to find the middle path and avoid excess</term>, which orders the use of all things perfectly according to the will of God, so that a person always has a heart estranged from all earthly things—so estranged that if the person loses earthly things, his heart becomes light and his soul so free and his senses so unburdened that he is just as happy in God as if his dearest friend had taken his heaviest burden from him. For whatever person finds that earthly things are not a heavy burden cannot be called a true spiritual person before God. Therefore our Lord spoke thus: In times of need one uses all things rightly, for good poverty is born of necessity; therefore it is holy, and in it excess can bring no darkness into the soul.

My other guardian is holy fear, which together with divine wisdom ensures that my soul cannot smile upon <note>original: "zůlachen," meaning to take delight in or look favorably upon</note> the earthly things given to it; rather, she receives them as if they were a temptation <note>original: "bekorunge," a trial or testing of one's virtue</note>, because of the dread of greed and vain honor, which [harms] many promised <note>Those who have taken religious vows or "professed" their faith</note> people in spiritual [life]...

# Sixth Part.

...living life so greatly darkened, that he has so certainly lost the light of discernment <term>discernment: "bescheidenheit," the ability to distinguish between good and evil or to find the middle path</term> and the fire of love <note>original: "miñe," the Middle High German term for mystical, courtly love, often used for the soul's relationship with God</note>, and the tasting of God’s sweetness, peace, and mercy, that he himself does not even know it.

Thus spoke our Lord: "Yes, they speak beautiful parables; they want to love earthly things and draw much to themselves under the pretense that they may serve me better; but they serve themselves more than me. The person who seeks his own comfort or benefit belongs to himself. But every person should be a Christ in himself, so that the person lives for God and not for himself. To the most blessed one, who lives entirely in God, it is all the same what he has, except for holy poverty <term>holy poverty: "helige armůte," a voluntary state of detachment from material and spiritual goods to mirror Christ</term>, into which God casts the person with His power, in the same way as He cast His most beloved Son down from heaven, onto the streets, into the manger of the inn; so our Lord still casts His chosen friends away from all earthly comfort, so that they may hunger for heavenly comfort. A truly holy person fears earthly good fortune more than he worries about earthly necessity. Why? Their dwelling is in the kingdom of heaven and their prison is in this world. Therefore," said our Lord, "whoever recognizes and loves the nobility of my freedom cannot endure to love me alone for my own sake, but he must love me in all creatures. Thus I remain the nearest in his soul."

Whenever I saw anything that was beautiful or dear to me, I began to sigh, then to weep, and then I began to think, to lament, and to speak thus to all things: "Oh no, now beware, for this is not your Beloved, who has touched your heart and enlightened your senses and bound your soul so delightfully, that this manifold sweetness of earthly things does not force you away from me." ### VI. In the final hour you shall have love, longing, fear, and a threefold repentance.

I asked my Lord how I should conduct myself at the final hour of my end. Then our Lord spoke: "You shall conduct yourself at the final hour just as you conducted yourself in the first hour. You shall maintain love <note>original: "miñe," the specific Middle High German term for mystical, divine love</note> and longing, repentance and fear, for these four things were the beginning of your life; therefore they must also be your end." Then I said: "Dear Lord; where then are the other two things, which are the foundation and the crown of heavenly glory—namely, Christian faith and true confidence <term>confidence: "zůversiht," a term implying both hope and a secure trust in God’s providence</term>?" Then our Lord spoke thus: "Your faith has become knowledge and your desire has been transformed into a true certainty." These interpretations <term>interpretations: "glosen," usually referring to explanatory notes on a text, but here meaning spiritual insights revealed by God</term> I saw in His words, and I also know them in my heart. My threefold repentance lies in three things.

I repent of my sins now most of all; this comes from love. But the pain of repentance I have lost in all-loving love. I repent for the sins of all people, so that it is for me just like a sick person who hungers for such a noble thing that cannot be theirs, or alas, only rarely. --- Therefore my heart must be sorrowful, and my soul hunts with its longing after the great wild animal <note>In medieval mystical literature, God is often depicted as a "great wild animal" or a stag that the soul must "hunt" through intense desire and prayer</note>. Regarding this, our Lord spoke: "One cannot catch the great animals unless one hunts them into a body of water. In the same way, no sinner is ever converted unless he is hunted by the urgent longing of holy people into the deep tears of their hearts."

I repent for all the good works that I have neglected because of my love of the flesh without true necessity. Regarding this, our Lord spoke: "One cannot build a dwelling unless one has a <note>The number ¹ refers to the footnote at the bottom: "Manuscript: iene."</note> ¹) steady place; likewise, one can receive no reward in the kingdom of heaven without the good deeds of good works." Our Lord grants this through heartfelt love, so that He may say to each soul: "Take, my most beloved, this manifold dignity <term>dignity: "wirdekeit," referring to the soul's spiritual rank or honor</term>; you have earned this yourself," so that God this word

# Part Six.

...[so that He] may speak with truth to the honor and love of the soul, just as if He were not the cause of her blessedness, and she may receive perfect honor in body and soul.

Therefore, our Lord has such heartfelt love for our labors, our poverty, and our sorrows, which we bear here in true love <note>original: "miñe," the specific Middle High German term for mystical, divine love</note>, that He sets aside His justice so nobly as ever befits His divinity <term>divinity: "gotheit," the essential nature of God</term>. This I have gathered from the heap of God's gifts.

->*VII. Our own will can resist the counter-hook. The good soul is swift toward God.*<-

In my company there is a spiritual person <term>spiritual person: "geistlich mensch," referring to a member of a religious order or a Beguine</term>, from whom I suffer much distress because of her bad habits, such that this person will not follow me in anything. I complained of this to God with all my longing and wondered greatly how this could be. Then our Lord spoke: "See what hinders it." Then I saw that a particular devil clung to the person and pulled her back from all good things <margin>She; the person was thus a woman.</margin>. Then I said: "Who has given you the power to offer God such great insults through this person?" Then the devil spoke: "No one has given me the power except for her own self-will <term>self-will: "mûtwille," a stubborn or intentional determination to follow one's own desires over God's</term>."

In these words I saw that the devil followed all spiritual people with such shameful mockery—those who give him permission within themselves by living deceitfully—so that he [the devil] excuses himself and all creatures before God. Then I said: "Who shall help this poor person to be delivered from you?" Then the devil spoke, compelled by God: "No one can help her but her own self-will; for God has given her the power that she may turn her mind around. When she does that, I must hurry away from her." — Now I ask you in the eternal truth: what is your name? Then the devil spoke: "My name is the Counter-hook <note>original: "widerhak," implying a snag or an obstacle that pulls one backward</note>, and this host that you see behind me are all my companions of this same office <term>office: "ambahte," a specific duty or role, often used in a feudal or administrative context</term> that I have; and there are as many of them as there are of us...

...find many people who do not want to follow their faithful guidance in good things.

Because of this, my soul became so swift toward God that she truly lifted herself up without any labor of her own, and she wrapped herself rightly in the Holy Trinity <term>Holy Trinity: "heilige drivaltekeit," the Christian doctrine of one God in three persons</term>, just as a child wraps itself in its mother's cloak and lays itself rightly against her breast. Then my soul spoke with the power and with the voice of all creatures thus: "Ah, most beloved, now consider my distress regarding this person, so that You, Lord, may transform her senses with Your divine sweetness." — "No," said our Lord, "she is not worthy of My sweetness; instead, I will make her body sick, so that she becomes so lame from the pain that she no longer walks the paths of sin. And I will make her so mute that she shall remain silent toward wicked words. She shall also become so blind that she is ashamed to look upon vanity. But whatever is then done to her, is done to Me." Truly, this came to pass within fourteen days. Alleluia <note>original: "Alleluja," an expression of praise</note>.

## *VIII. Between God and Lucifer there are two kinds of purgatory <term>purgatory: "vegefůr," a place of purification for souls before entering heaven</term>. How the devil torments souls.*

Our human brother Jesus Christ has ascended with all virtues into the heights of His divinity, and no one may follow Him there unless they also possess all virtues in a like manner, just as the Holy Trinity has honorably established itself above all things in the blissful heights with all His virtuous friends; thereafter they are eternally honorable, beautiful, and joyful, as they bring with them the praiseworthy likeness of His divine virtues. Indeed, every virtue that is performed here on earth with good will and without deceit, adorned with love <note>original: "miñe," the specific Middle High German term for mystical, divine love</note> and completed without sin—those are the strings in the kingdom of heaven that sound forever without end from the faithful soul and from the willing body into the Holy Trinity. There, the Father thanks His Son that He has drawn the soul there with virtues, and the Son honors the Father for having created her, and the Holy Spirit so tenderly compels the Father and the Son that the Holy Trinity flows so powerfully toward her and sings so sweetly that she intends and loves all things with God.

### Sixth Part.

So the sinful devil Lucifer has sunk below all things with all those alone who love and intend wickedness. Between God’s height and the devil’s abyss there are still two kinds of purgatory <term>purgatory: "vegefür," a place of purification where souls are cleansed of sin before entering heaven</term>. In these two purgatories there is many a kind of pain and distress. The first purgatory is the useful sorrow that we suffer in this world in manifold pains. The other purgatory, after this life, is so great that it begins before the mouth of hell and ends before the gate of heaven. But the devils may not torment the souls further than on the earth, in the air, and in all those places where the person has sinned, and in all the heights where he has disturbed the air with his sins. By this, the devil points them out <note>original: "erzüget," meaning to demonstrate or showcase</note>, so that their shame and their pain may be all the greater for all the sins that remained unatoned for here.

But when they become so blessed that they are ever released from the devil’s hands, they then move themselves through the pain with little distress. Thereafter they come, with help and through suffering, over all distress; that is, so near to the kingdom of heaven that they have every joy, except for three kinds of joy they do not yet have: That they do not see God, that they have not received their honor, and that they have not been crowned. Thus is purgatory upon the earth and in the air between hell and the kingdom of heaven. But it is in a spiritual manner such that the soul can suffer no pain from earthly things once it comes from this body.

## IX. Whoever honors the saints, they honor and comfort him at his death.

That one honors the saints with beautiful remembrance <term>remembrance: "gehūgenisse," a liturgical or meditative calling to mind</term> and with all the devotion that one may have on the day when God honored them with a holy end—this is so pleasing to them that they come present there with all the lordship that they have received from their piety. This I saw truly on Saint Mary Magdalene’s day, when God was honored with praiseworthy song because of the great honor she has received as a reward. She stepped <note>original: "schrikete," meaning to move suddenly or glide</note> into the choir following the holy song, and she looked every singer in the eyes, and she stepped forward and spoke: "To the end of all those who honor my end, I will come, and I will honor them in return; everything according to what they are able to receive, so will I..."

# Chapters IX — X.

I let them enter. Four great archangels led her between them, and the number of lesser angels was beyond human counting. Then I asked what the four princes were named. She spoke: The first is named Strength, the second is named Yearning <note>original: "gerunge," referring to the intense spiritual desire for God</note>, the third is named Good Will, and the fourth is named Steadfastness. "Because with these four virtues I have overcome all my heart's sorrow, God has given me as a reward both service, dominion, and a crown." It is the same for other saints. Then our Lord spoke: "When one blows upon the smallest spark, it gives off heat and light in the heavenly fire, wherein the burning saints dwell."

### X. *Prayer, masses, the word of God, the lives of good people, fasting, and Lenten penance release souls from purgatory.*

I prayed for a soul; the body had been murdered while living in sin. Then our Lord spoke thus: "Seven years of fasting and seven Lenten penances <term>carreñe: a specific period of strict penance, often forty days of bread and water</term> would be like a raindrop in a great fire. Within thirty years he shall not be ransomed from Me through prayer, for he lost his life through foolish pride thirty years before his time; for this, he must pay Me in his distress." The soul spoke: "O Lord, yes, but he <note>original manuscript reads: "en"</note> may still wait upon Your goodness." God spoke: "Indeed, where two wrestle with one another, the weakest must go under. I will be the weaker one, even though I am Almighty." Three thousand masses are his ransom, because he never heard a full mass unless he did it out of shame <note>meaning he only attended church to avoid social embarrassment or to keep up appearances</note>. (The soul asked): "Lord, by what means was he saved?" — (God replied): "Whenever he heard My word, he sighed; I rewarded him for this, because when he last lived, he sighed for his sins." — "Lord, if his mother’s brother, who has been a spiritual man <term>geistlich man: a member of the clergy or a monk</term> from his youth even to his grey hair, with many labors and troubles—if he offered that for him, and went out and placed himself in the state where he was first known for your love, would You not set the soul free?" — "Yes," spoke our Lord. "If I were so greatly pressured, I would have to give everything that was desired." — "Lord, if the spiritual man gave his good works to the poor soul, what should then happen to it?"

<header>Part Six.</header>

Immediately God let me see the blessed one,
Which could not happen to me before
Because of his unchaste <note>original: "unkůsche," here likely referring to the impurity of suffering or the state of the soul before purification</note> suffering, which my soul cannot endure.
Then he was fairer than the sun,
And he floated in clear bliss
High above all earthly misery.
Then he spoke joyfully and was very glad:
"Tell my friends: even if the earth were golden
And the bright sun within it
Shone without ceasing,
Both day and night,
And with it the sweet air of May,
Beautiful flowers with full fruit—
Even then, I would not wish to be there for a single hour,
So wonderfully blissful is this life."

Yet he had not yet entered the eternal heaven.

### *XI. How a student is dead and a preacher.*

Thus speaks our Lord: "I tell you by my burning divinity and by my living humanity, that his nature is dead to a holy death, so that he nevermore commits mortal sin <term>hőptsůnde: a "head" or capital sin that leads to spiritual death</term> upon earth." Then he was seen looking like a preacher, and he stood upon a red marble pillar and preached to the people thus:

> "Come, you blessed of my Father <note>original: *Venite benedicti patris mei*</note>. Come to me, all you who are blessed, and depart from me, all you who are unblessed."

Then it was seen and known that all preachers preach and teach us from these two words.

### *XII. How you should conduct yourself in fourteen things.*

When you pray, you should make yourself small
With great humility.
When you confess, you should be truthful.
When you perform your penance, you should be diligent.
When you eat, you should be moderate <note>original: "gnůgig," meaning content with what is sufficient</note>.
When you sleep, you should be disciplined.
When you are alone, you should be faithful.
When you are among people, you should be wise.
When you are taught good manners, you should be obedient.
When your wickedness is rebuked, you should be patient.
When you do anything good, you should think yourself base. ¹)
When you do evil, you should immediately seek grace.

## *XIII. How spiritual people, out of blindness, guard themselves against divine love. Concerning the sixfold power of God’s gifts.*

O dear Jesus, God of the heavenly kingdom; Lord, I must ask you one thing, for I can no longer endure the great blindness that I recognize in this matter. It is this: that spiritual people <note>original: "geistliche lúte," referring to those in holy orders or religious life</note> guard themselves against divine inwardness <term>iñekeit: a state of deep, internal devotion and focus on God</term> in this way: whenever God deigns to let His divine heart blossom with love <term>miñe: the specific medieval concept of "Minne," or ecstatic, reciprocal love between the soul and God</term> toward the most blessed soul—so much so that a small spark flies out toward the cold soul and kindles it so much that the person's heart begins to burn, their soul to melt, and their eyes to flow—then our Lord would gladly make an earthly human so heavenly that one could truly follow, love, and know God through them. But then human reason speaks: "No, I can be more useful in external things." This is especially what cloistered people <note>original: "closterlůte," monastics</note> say when they think they are being most wise. — To this, our Lord answers thus:

> My divinity came into the earthly realm, my humanity did the toil;
> My divinity stepped upon the cross, my humanity suffered death;
> My divinity rose from death and led humanity into heaven.
> All those who drive me out from themselves,
> They shall be driven away from me.
> What can a human do in themselves?
> Nothing more than sin!
> Since humanity never fully accomplished anything,
> Except alone what my divinity pre-ordained.
> They say: "Lord, it is wisdom,
> That one should spare the body," when your divine breath,
> Which from your holy Trinity
> Swings down so sweetly
> And penetrates so powerfully through the soul,
> That the body loses all its strength,
> Then the person is incapacitated <note>original: "vnberhaft," meaning the body is rendered powerless or unproductive by the intensity of the spiritual experience</note>.

Our Lord says this: One should not set the King’s food aside in vain, before one has eaten of earthly necessity. My special gift brings special dignity to the human being.

# Part Six.

...in soul and in body. It <note>Refers to the divine gift or grace mentioned on the previous page.</note> gives teaching to the foolish and comfort to the wise. It also gives eternal praise and endless honor to the bottomless well-spring from which it flowed out, when it swings back upward with full fruit to the place from whence it flowed down to me. Indeed, the grace that God is accustomed to give to a person with power and searching inquiry is in itself so noble, and it comes with such great friendship from God, that the person does not commit even a small sin that would turn them away from Him for the sake of transitory things. Oh alas, ignoble soul, how can you endure that you turn God away from you before you have properly enjoyed Him according to His will? For His highest delight <note>original: "wollust," here meaning spiritual bliss or divine pleasure</note> is hidden within you. Do you want to know how you should use the holy gift of God and consume it according to God’s will?

> Yes, she <note>Grace/The Gift is personified as a female guide.</note> shall teach it to you herself,
> If she is welcome to you. With outward virtues
> And with inward longing you shall receive her;
> With humble fear you shall keep her,
> Subject in all needs.
> Give her time and place within you,
> She asks for nothing else.
> She shall melt you so deep into God,
> That you recognize His will,
> How long you should follow
> His necessary endearment <term>trůtunge: a mystical term for God’s tender, intimate caressing of the soul</term> within yourself
> And at what time and how you should work
> For the sinners and for those who are in purgatory.
> And look upon the needs of every person,
> Whether they be living or dead.
> &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;When you have accomplished this
> Inwardly according to God’s delight
> And according to the power of your soul,
> For she becomes weary in herself
> While she is bound in her mortal body.
> After this enjoyment <note>original: "gebruchunge," refers to the "fruitful use" or mystical enjoyment of God's presence</note>, the soul speaks thus:
> Lord, flee now from me inwardly
> And stand by me outwardly,
> So that all my works sink according to your gift
> And I gladly lament of necessity and trouble.

### XIV. Those who lament in their pain lack six things. How one should seek to bear humiliation.

->Whoever complains of their hindrance in pain,<-
->Is blind in their knowledge,<-

Therefore our Lord spoke thus: "A person does not want to be sick and does not want to be despised, and upon what then shall I bind his honor?" — "Lord, when a person is sick and despised, with what shall he then build honor?" — "When he is sick, he shall honor, serve, and love me solely with joyful patience; when he is despised, he shall love me and be patient; for the preachers <note>original: "predier"</note> and the confessors <note>original: "bihter"</note> are so constrained by their office <note>original: "ambahte"</note> that they cannot practice it [the contemplative life], and yet they have holy will; that is not a hindrance to their salvation, it is an adornment for their halos <term>aureolen: a special heavenly crown or radiance believed to be granted to martyrs, virgins, and teachers</term>."

### XV. *Of the pain of Enoch and Elijah and of the last preachers and of the wickedness of the Antichrist.*

Oh powerful love of God <note>original: "gotzmiñe"</note>, you have laid such sweet distress upon me that my soul suffers from wonder. Whenever I think that my body must be extinguished in death, so that I shall no longer suffer or praise my dear Jesus, it is so painful to me that I then desire, if it were possible, to live until the last day. The faithful love <note>original: "miñe"</note> that is God's within me and not my own constrains me to this. Therefore our Lord spoke: "If you must die, let all your time be a cause of regret to you, however holy you may be." Oh Lord, I pray you, that my longing <note>original: "gerunge"</note> does not die when I can no longer achieve anything with my body. Then our Lord spoke: "Your longing shall live, for it cannot die, because it is eternal. If it works thus for me until the end of time, then soul and body shall come together again. Then I will set them back in, then it shall praise me without end, and it has served me from the first beginning, for you would have wished to have existed with Adam until now for my love; thus you would have wished to fulfill all people's sorrow and all people's service for me. I say more: Your being shall stand until the last human being."

<summary>The text concludes a teaching on maintaining virtue through sickness and social contempt, then transitions to a mystical reflection on the soul's desire to serve God until the end of time.</summary>

### Part Six.

> Oh, my dearly beloved,
> How shall the last person be,
> To whom my life should conform?
> For the life of spiritual people
> Will be very scarce at the end of the world.

Thus our Lord answered: Enoch shall be the last person who shall cultivate a spiritual life. After this, God showed me the end of this world again, and when the last brothers are to be martyred in this way: Their hair, which they are never to cut, is provided by a special decree of the will of God; the Antichrist <note>original: "endecrist"</note> commands that they be hanged from trees by this hair. There they hang and die most beautifully, for their hearts burn within from the sweet heavenly fire <term>heavenly fire: a common mystical metaphor for the intense, purifying warmth of divine love felt in the soul</term> as intensely as the body suffers in its distress. Therefore, between the comfort of the Holy Spirit and the agony of the poor flesh, their souls depart from their bodies without any of the terror usually caused by pain.

Elijah <note>original: "Helyas"</note> and Enoch travel from India as far as the sea, and a great multitude follows each of them; these are all Christian people who flee from the Antichrist to join them. They are all beaten to death in the same way that one hunts mad dogs in the street. They are forgiven their sins and may live no longer. Then others follow them who are secret Christians, for they recognize well from God that they cannot otherwise escape the state of unbelief. Elijah is martyred first of all; he is bound to a high cross and nailed through his hands.

> They do this out of grim hatred,
> Because he ever spoke of the saints
> And of what happened to Christ there.
> They do not grant him a quick death,

> That he might renounce the Christian teaching
> And thus turn to the Antichrist.
> Then the holy favorite of God <term>favorite of God: "gotztrut," a Middle High German term for one specially beloved by or intimate with God</term> stands,
> And he never cries out in his pain.
> He comforts holy Christendom
> For three days and three nights
> Until his soul departs.
> I saw the Heavenly Father at his end,
> And He received Elijah’s soul

# Chapter XV.

With his human hands, ¹)
And he spoke: "Come, my dear, it is time for you!"
And in a heavenly flash, God led him away.

The wretched man, the Antichrist <note>original: "endecrist"</note>, does not permit the beloved of God <note>original: "gotztrut"</note> to be buried, because he wishes for all the Christians to despair. In this, however, he is deceived; for all who look upon the body are moved toward the Christian faith and feel a desire to worship him, because they become so full of sweetness from the presence of the holy body that they forget the torment of death and all earthly goods.

> Enoch still lives then,
> For the Antichrist desires
> To hear all the wisdom
> That Enoch knows from God,
> So that he might openly distort it
> With his false teaching;
> And if he could draw Enoch to his side,
> Then the whole world would be his with great honor.

Meanwhile, so many of the wicked are drawn by the Antichrist that he assails Enoch with grim words, and only then does Enoch tell the Antichrist the whole truth, thus:

> You are a scourge <term>scourge: a whip or instrument of punishment; used here to explain that the Antichrist is a tool permitted by God to punish the wicked and refine the holy</term> to all the world,
> Sent by God because of the wickedness of the evil
> And for the holiness of the good.
> You know well the scriptures of the Old Law <note>The Old Testament</note>
> And just as well the New Law <note>The New Testament</note>,
> Now see how you shall prosper according to your works.
> To this you have diligently chosen:
> According to the scripture, you must be lost.
> That you can read well for yourself.
> You also did not create the earth nor the heavens,
> You do not give the angels eternal life,
> You have not made mankind,
> Neither soul nor body.
> You have never given any creature
> Its natural body;
> How then could you be God?
> Your works are all with lies and false cunning;
> The eternal truth is Jesus Christ,
> Who is one eternal God with his Father.

### Sixth Part

->The Antichrist speaks with wrath:<-

> How dare you take my enemy from before me,
> To whom you attribute my honor over me?
> I will satisfy myself upon you
> And I will release the whole world from you.
> Take him quickly by my power
> And pour boiling pitch into his mouth
> And bind his neck tightly,
> So that my enemy will be silenced at once.
> If I could [still] hear his words,
> I would gladly let him suffer longer.
> Hang him, thus dead, high above all murderers,
> So that all who look upon him
> Shall abandon the Christian faith.
> He has spoken against my honor;
> No one needs his teaching.
> I have long been foreseen;
> It shall go for me according to my teaching. ¹)

Enoch speaks his holy prayer in his heart thus:

> Eternal Father and Son and Holy Spirit,
> You eternal, inseparable God,
> I thank you, Lord, for your long choosing of me <note>Enoch was "chosen" to be taken up to Heaven alive and preserved for centuries specifically to return and face the Antichrist.</note>
> And I praise you, Lord, now in this torment <note>original: "qwelunge"</note>.
> I pray to you, Lord, for your sheep and mine,
> Who now remain without a shepherd;
> Keep them, Lord, especially,
> And comfort them secretly.
> Now receive, Lord, my soul.
> I have no earthly love for my body.

The answer that God will then give him, and His thanks and His prayer, which is written here—this I saw and I read it written in the Holy Trinity <note>The author claims a mystical vision, seeing these words written within the essence of the Trinity itself.</note> thus:

> Dear son, now hurry quickly to me;
> I am truly in you.
> Your friends, for whom you have prayed to me,
> Shall themselves baptize their children;
> I will soon release them from the Antichrist.
> They shall remain Christians in their hearts,
> And I will guard them from all doubt.
> Come, dear beloved <note>original: "trut"</note>, I wait for you,
> And my heart leaps toward you.

### Chapter XVI.

*XVI. How the soul of our Lord dwells in the Trinity and concerning its office. How it speaks for the sinner, and concerning the office of our Lady.*

As I wake in the night,
I test my power with wisdom
To see if I, poor soul, might pray
For this unfaithful Christendom <note>Mechthild frequently critiques the religious failings of her contemporary world.</note>,
Which causes my body so much pain.
Sometimes He <note>God</note> draws me another way,
Without bridge or path,
Where I must follow Him, naked and barefoot,
Away from all human things.
Who can so gently subdue humanity <note>original: "menscheit"</note>?
Who can so gently uplift the soul?
Who can so highly enlighten the senses,
Except for God, who created them?
He performs wondrous deeds with us.
And so, in one night, I thought
Upon the Holy Trinity,
With the sweet flowing <note>original: "vlusse"; a central concept in Mechthild’s work, *The Flowing Light of the Godhead*</note> of my soul, without labor.
Then I saw in the heights of the entire Holy Trinity
The soul of our Lord Jesus, unadorned.
His soul dwells constantly above all dignity
Within the Holy Trinity;
There it is enclosed and wondrously wrought,
And it shines honorably above the beauty of all creatures
Through the three holy persons.
Then I desired with great modesty <note>original: "gezogenheit," referring to the formal etiquette of a royal court</note>,
As is the custom at court,
That I might speak to His honor
With the soul of our Lord,
For it seemed to me that it performed special wonders.

Then I hovered so near to it that I greeted it thus: "Blessed are you, most beloved! What wonders you perform in this eternal mirror <note>The "mirror" is a common medieval metaphor for the Trinity or the divine essence where everything is reflected.</note>, in which all the blessed behold themselves so wondrously! You have sweet labor in blissful unrest <note>original: "vnrůwe"; the paradox of the soul being active yet at peace in God.</note>." Then the soul of our Lord spoke to the lowly one <note>original: "snỏden"; Mechthild’s term for her own soul's unworthiness</note> thus:

> Welcome, my likeness,
> For I am also a soul, even as you are,
> And I have borne the burden of all souls
> With my innocent body.
> This is my office <term>office: "ambaht" – a specialized duty, service, or liturgical role</term>.

<sig>H. Mechthild. 13</sig>

<summary>Mechthild describes a mystical night-vision where she is drawn into the Holy Trinity and encounters the soul of Jesus. The dialogue emphasizes the shared nature between the human soul and Christ's soul, which serves the "office" of bearing humanity's burdens.</summary>
